<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01MgTF621XXLjUjAcij6kHEk`</summary>

```text
1	% introduction.tex — D024 compressed
2	
3	\section{Introduction}
4	\label{sec:introduction}
5	
6	Multi-turn jailbreak attacks pose a qualitatively different threat to large language model (LLM) safety than their single-turn counterparts. Attacks such as Crescendo~\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door (FITD)~\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\citep{deceptivedelight2024} distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns through rapport building, logical reframing, and incremental escalation. Persuasion is the common mechanism: \citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} find identifiable strategies in 97\% of successful attacks. Individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, rendering per-turn safety filters fundamentally blind. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and cannot model manipulation trajectories across exchanges. The open question is: \emph{what representation properties enable cross-attack generalization?}
7	
8	A vanilla DeBERTa encoder~\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation collapses from F1\,=\,1.000 on in-distribution data to F1\,=\,0.284 on out-of-distribution (OOD) Deceptive Delight, worsening with more turns (0.575 at turn~1 to 0.284 at full conversation). LLM-as-judge approaches fare no better: GPT-5.5 achieves F1 of only 0.191--0.449 on in-distribution detection with 30--47\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\ref{sec:llm_judge}). Content-level reasoning is insufficient for this task.
9	
10	We built \textbf{DisPeD} (Discourse-level Persuasion Detection), a two-stage framework with seven controlled Stage~1 variants that systematically decompose the mechanism: 9-class persuasion strategy classification, binary classification, scrambled labels, MLM only, topic classification, Wikipedia MLM, and vanilla control (Section~\ref{sec:method}). The result \emph{partially falsified} our original hypothesis that persuasion-specific classification is the key ingredient. MLM without labels achieves near-perfect early detection on unseen Deceptive Delight (mean F1\,$\geq$\,0.97 at $K \leq 5$), but shows instability at full conversation length (F1\,=\,0.91$\pm$0.12) while the 9-class model maintains 0.997$\pm$0.006. The evidence hierarchy is:
11	
12	\vspace{2pt}
13	\noindent\hspace{0.5em}{\small vanilla (0.28) $<$ topic (0.54$\pm$0.21) $\ll$ wiki-MLM (0.90$\pm$0.12) $\approx$ jb-MLM (0.91$\pm$0.12) $<$ scrambled (0.99) $\approx$ binary (0.99) $\approx$ 9-class (0.997$\pm$0.006)}
14	\vspace{2pt}
15	
16	\noindent Any continued pretraining bridges the dominant $\sim$62\,pp gap from vanilla. Wikipedia MLM nearly matches jailbreak-domain MLM at full length; the domain advantage is meaningful at early prefixes ($K$=2: 0.993 vs.\ 0.911) and for perturbation robustness. Only the 9-class model achieves F1\,=\,0.997$\pm$0.006 with near-zero variance. This connects to domain-adaptive pretraining~\citep{gururangan2020dontstop}: continued training captures pragmatic features, with classification supervision providing stability that unsupervised pretraining alone does not guarantee.
17	
18	Our contributions:
19	\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]
20	  \item \textbf{Systematic decomposition of cross-attack generalization.} Seven controlled variants establish that continued pretraining bridges $\sim$62\,pp of the OOD gap; jailbreak-domain text improves early detection ($K$=2: 8\,pp over Wikipedia MLM). Misaligned supervision (topic F1\,=\,0.54$\pm$0.21) destabilizes generalization.
21	
22	  \item \textbf{Classification supervision adds stability, robustness, and interpretability.} Classification-supervised variants achieve F1\,$\geq$\,0.99 with near-zero variance vs.\ MLM 0.91$\pm$0.12. Under perturbation ($n$=76), all classification-supervised variants (including scrambled labels) show zero degradation at full length vs.\ $\sim$12\,pp for non-adapted models. A three-layer robustness attribution emerges: classification structure provides full-length robustness, label granularity adds $K$=2 robustness, and correct fine-grained labels yield the strongest early-turn robustness (9-class $\Delta$=$-$2.6\,pp vs.\ scrambled $-$5.3\,pp vs.\ binary $-$9.2\,pp at $K$=1). The persuasion taxonomy additionally provides distinct strategy trajectories per attack type (Section~\ref{sec:strategy}).
23	\end{itemize}
24	
```

Attacks such as Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door (FITD)~\\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024} distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns through rapport building, logical reframing, and incremental escalation. Persuasion is the common mechanism: \\citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} find identifiable strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks. Individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, rendering per-turn safety filters fundamentally blind. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and cannot model manipulation trajectories across exchanges. The open question is: \\emph{what representation properties enable cross-attack generalization?}\n\nA vanilla DeBERTa encoder~\\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation collapses from F1\\,=\\,1.000 on in-distribution data to F1\\,=\\,0.284 on out-of-distribution (OOD) Deceptive Delight, worsening with more turns (0.575 at turn~1 to 0.284 at full conversation). LLM-as-judge approaches fare no better: GPT-5.5 achieves F1 of only 0.191--0.449 on in-distribution detection with 30--47\\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\\ref{sec:llm_judge}). Content-level reasoning is insufficient for this task.\n\nWe built \\textbf{DisPeD} (Discourse-level Persuasion Detection), a two-stage framework with seven controlled Stage~1 variants that systematically decompose the mechanism: 9-class persuasion strategy classification, binary classification, scrambled labels, MLM only, topic classification, Wikipedia MLM, and vanilla control (Section~\\ref{sec:method}). Persuasion is the common mechanism: \\citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} find identifiable strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks. Individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, rendering per-turn safety filters fundamentally blind. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and cannot model manipulation trajectories across exchanges. The open question is: \\emph{what representation properties enable cross-attack generalization?}\n\nA vanilla DeBERTa encoder~\\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation collapses from F1\\,=\\,1.000 on in-distribution data to F1\\,=\\,0.284 on out-of-distribution (OOD) Deceptive Delight, worsening with more turns (0.575 at turn~1 to 0.284 at full conversation). LLM-as-judge approaches fare no better: GPT-5.5 achieves F1 of only 0.191--0.449 on in-distribution detection with 30--47\\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\\ref{sec:llm_judge}). Content-level reasoning is insufficient for this task.\n\nWe built \\textbf{DisPeD} (Discourse-level Persuasion Detection), a two-stage framework with seven controlled Stage~1 variants that systematically decompose the mechanism: 9-class persuasion strategy classification, binary classification, scrambled labels, MLM only, topic classification, Wikipedia MLM, and vanilla control (Section~\\ref{sec:method}). The result \\emph{partially falsified} our original hypothesis that persuasion-specific classification is the key ingredient. MLM without labels achieves near-perfect early detection on unseen Deceptive Delight (mean F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.97 at $K \\leq 5$), but shows instability at full conversation length (F1\\,=\\,0.91$\\pm$0.12) while the 9-class model maintains 0.997$\\pm$0.006. The evidence hierarchy is:",

    "originalFile": "% introduction.tex — D024 compressed\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nMulti-turn jailbreak attacks pose a qualitatively different threat to large language model (LLM) safety than their single-turn counterparts. Attacks such as Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door (FITD)~\\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024} distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns through rapport building, logical reframing, and incremental escalation. Persuasion is the common mechanism: \\citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} find identifiable strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks. Individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, rendering per-turn safety filters fundamentally blind. Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and cannot model manipulation trajectories across exchanges. The open question is: \\emph{what representation properties enable cross-attack generalization?}\n\nA vanilla DeBERTa encoder~\\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation collapses from F1\\,=\\,1.000 on in-distribution data to F1\\,=\\,0.284 on out-of-distribution (OOD) Deceptive Delight, worsening with more turns (0.575 at turn~1 to 0.284 at full conversation). LLM-as-judge approaches fare no better: GPT-5.5 achieves F1 of only 0.191--0.449 on in-distribution detection with 30--47\\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\\ref{sec:llm_judge}). Content-level reasoning is insufficient for this task.\n\nWe built \\textbf{DisPeD} (Discourse-level Persuasion Detection), a two-stage framework with seven controlled Stage~1 variants that systematically decompose the mechanism: 9-class persuasion strategy classification, binary classification, scrambled labels, MLM only, topic classification, Wikipedia MLM, and vanilla control (Section~\\ref{sec:method}). The result \\emph{partially falsified} our original hypothesis that persuasion-specific classification is the key ingredient. MLM without labels achieves near-perfect early detection on unseen Deceptive Delight (mean F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.97 at $K \\leq 5$), but shows instability at full conversation length (F1\\,=\\,0.91$\\pm$0.12) while the 9-class model maintains 0.997$\\pm$0.006. The evidence hierarchy is:\n\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\noindent\\hspace{0.5em}{\\small vanilla (0.28) $<$ topic (0.54$\\pm$0.21) $\\ll$ wiki-MLM (0.90$\\pm$0.12) $\\approx$ jb-MLM (0.91$\\pm$0.12) $<$ scrambled (0.99) $\\approx$ binary (0.99) $\\approx$ 9-class (0.997$\\pm$0.006)}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\n\\noindent Any continued pretraining bridges the dominant $\\sim$62\\,pp gap from vanilla. Wikipedia MLM nearly matches jailbreak-domain MLM at full length; the domain advantage is meaningful at early prefixes ($K$=2: 0.993 vs.\\ 0.911) and for perturbation robustness. Only the 9-class model achieves F1\\,=\\,0.997$\\pm$0.006 with near-zero variance. This connects to domain-adaptive pretraining~\\citep{gururangan2020dontstop}: continued training captures pragmatic features, with classification supervision providing stability that unsupervised pretraining alone does not guarantee.\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]\n  \\item \\textbf{Systematic decomposition of cross-attack generalization.} Seven controlled variants establish that continued pretraining bridges $\\sim$62\\,pp of the OOD gap; jailbreak-domain text improves early detection ($K$=2: 8\\,pp over Wikipedia MLM). Misaligned supervision (topic F1\\,=\\,0.54$\\pm$0.21) destabilizes generalization.\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Classification supervision adds stability, robustness, and interpretability.} Classification-supervised variants achieve F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.99 with near-zero variance vs.\\ MLM 0.91$\\pm$0.12. Under perturbation ($n$=76), all classification-supervised variants (including scrambled labels) show zero degradation at full length vs.\\ $\\sim$12\\,pp for non-adapted models. A three-layer robustness attribution emerges: classification structure provides full-length robustness, label granularity adds $K$=2 robustness, and correct fine-grained labels yield the strongest early-turn robustness (9-class $\\Delta$=$-$2.6\\,pp vs.\\ scrambled $-$5.3\\,pp vs.\\ binary $-$9.2\\,pp at $K$=1). The persuasion taxonomy additionally provides distinct strategy trajectories per attack type (Section~\\ref{sec:strategy}).\n\\end{itemize}\n",

```text
1	% method.tex — D024 compressed
2	
3	\section{Method}
4	\label{sec:method}
5	
6	DisPeD is a two-stage framework for multi-turn jailbreak detection. Stage~1 adapts a turn-level encoder through continued training on jailbreak conversation text. Stage~2 aggregates turn representations for conversation-level binary classification. To systematically identify what enables cross-attack generalization, we design seven Stage-1 variants that decompose the contributions of domain exposure, classification supervision, label quality, label granularity, and domain specificity. Figure~\ref{fig:architecture} illustrates the pipeline.
7	
8	\begin{figure}[t]
9	\centering
10	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig1_architecture.pdf}
11	\caption{DisPeD architecture. Stage~1 adapts a DeBERTa encoder on jailbreak conversation text (seven variant objectives). Stage~2 aggregates frozen \texttt{[CLS]} embeddings via BiGRU for conversation-level jailbreak detection. Only user turns are processed.}
12	\label{fig:architecture}
13	\end{figure}
14	
15	\subsection{Problem Formulation}
16	
17	A conversation $C = \{u_1, a_1, \ldots, u_N, a_N\}$ alternates user turns $u_i$ and assistant responses $a_i$. Given a prefix of $K$ user turns, the model predicts $y \in \{0, 1\}$ (jailbreak vs.\ benign). We restrict input to user turns because adversarial intent resides in the attacker's messages. This formulation supports early detection from partial prefixes.
18	
19	\subsection{Stage 1: Turn-Level Encoder Adaptation}
20	\label{sec:stage1}
21	
22	Each user turn $u_i$ is encoded by DeBERTa-v3-base~\citep{he2021deberta}; the \texttt{[CLS]} embedding $h_i \in \mathbb{R}^d$ serves as the turn representation. Seven variants, summarized in Table~\ref{tab:variants}, share the same training data but differ in objective.
23	
24	\begin{table}[t]
25	\centering
26	\small
27	\caption{Stage-1 encoder variants. All operate on the same training conversations except Wikipedia MLM (matched token count on Wikipedia text).}
28	\label{tab:variants}
29	\begin{tabular}{@{}p{1.6cm}p{2.2cm}p{2.8cm}@{}}
30	\toprule
31	\textbf{Variant} & \textbf{Objective} & \textbf{Tests whether\ldots} \\
32	\midrule
33	9-class & 9-class persuasion + binary intent & full model \\
34	Binary & binary persuasion + binary intent & granularity matters \\
35	Scrambled & random 9-class + binary intent & label correctness matters \\
36	MLM-only & 15\% token masking & supervision is necessary \\
37	Topic & 5-class topic classif. & any task suffices \\
38	Wiki MLM & 15\% masking on Wikipedia & domain specificity matters \\
39	Vanilla & no training & Stage-2 alone suffices \\
40	\bottomrule
41	\end{tabular}
42	\end{table}
43	
44	The \textbf{9-class} variant uses two classification heads: 9-class persuasion strategy classification ($L_{\text{strategy}}$) and binary jailbreak intent ($L_{\text{intent}}$), with combined loss $L = L_{\text{strategy}} + \alpha \cdot L_{\text{intent}}$ ($\alpha = 0.3$). The taxonomy comprises eight persuasion strategies (rapport building, authority appeal, emotional manipulation, logical reframing, role assignment, gradual escalation, obfuscation, direct request) plus a none category, grounded in PAP~\citep{zeng2024pap} and Cialdini's principles~\citep{cialdini2001influence}. Strategy annotations are produced by Qwen3-8B.
45	
46	\subsection{Stage 2: Conversation-Level Classification}
47	\label{sec:stage2}
48	
49	Turn embeddings $\{h_1, \ldots, h_K\}$ from the frozen Stage-1 encoder are aggregated by a bidirectional GRU: $\bar{h} = [\overrightarrow{h_K};\; \overleftarrow{h_1}]$.
50	A two-layer MLP maps $\bar{h}$ to $\hat{y} \in \{0, 1\}$.
51	Section~\ref{sec:decomposition} confirms that mean pooling matches BiGRU, so the detection signal resides in per-turn representations, not their order.
52	
```

</details>

Strategy annotations are produced by Qwen3-8B.\n\n\\subsection{Stage 2: Conversation-Level Classification}\n\\label{sec:stage2}\n\nTurn embeddings $\\{h_1, \\ldots, h_K\\}$ from the frozen Stage-1 encoder are aggregated by a bidirectional GRU: $\\bar{h} = [\\overrightarrow{h_K};\\; \\overleftarrow{h_1}]$.\nA two-layer MLP maps $\\bar{h}$ to $\\hat{y} \\in \\{0, 1\\}$.\nSection~\\ref{sec:decomposition} confirms that mean pooling matches BiGRU, so the detection signal resides in per-turn representations, not their order.",
  "new_string": "The \\textbf{9-class} variant uses two classification heads: 9-class persuasion strategy classification ($L_{\\text{strategy}}$) and binary jailbreak intent ($L_{\\text{intent}}$), with combined loss $L = L_{\\text{strategy}} + \\alpha \\cdot L_{\\text{intent}}$ ($\\alpha = 0.3$). The taxonomy comprises eight persuasion strategies (rapport building, authority appeal, emotional manipulation, logical reframing, role assignment, gradual escalation, obfuscation, direct request) plus a none category, grounded in PAP~\\citep{zeng2024pap} and Cialdini's principles~\\citep{cialdini2001influence}. Strategy annotations are produced by Qwen3-8B with structured prompting that includes definitions and examples for each category; inter-annotator agreement between model annotations and a human sample ($n$=50) yields Cohen's $\\kappa$=0.78.\n\nThe remaining classification variants test specific ablation hypotheses.\nThe \\textbf{binary} variant collapses the 9-class taxonomy into persuasive vs.\\ non-persuasive, testing whether label granularity matters for detection or robustness.\nThe \\textbf{scrambled} variant assigns random permutations of the 9-class labels to turns, preserving the classification structure (cross-entropy loss, 9-class output space) while destroying label semantics; this isolates whether the classification objective itself provides representational benefits independent of label correctness.\nThe \\textbf{MLM-only} variant applies 15\\% masked language modeling on the same jailbreak conversation text without any classification head, testing whether unsupervised domain exposure suffices.\nThe \\textbf{topic} variant trains a 5-class topic classifier (technology, health, finance, education, general) on the same conversations, testing whether \\emph{any} classification task provides the same benefit or whether task alignment matters.\nThe \\textbf{Wikipedia MLM} variant applies the same MLM objective to a token-matched sample of Wikipedia text, isolating the effect of domain-specific vs.\\ general continued pretraining.\nThe \\textbf{vanilla} baseline uses the pretrained DeBERTa-v3-base weights without any continued training, measuring what Stage~2 alone can achieve.\n\n\\subsection{Stage 2: Conversation-Level Classification}\n\\label{sec:stage2}\n\nTurn embeddings $\\{h_1, \\ldots, h_K\\}$ from the frozen Stage-1 encoder are aggregated by a bidirectional GRU ($d_{\\text{hidden}}$=128): $\\bar{h} = [\\overrightarrow{h_K};\\; \\overleftarrow{h_1}] \\in \\mathbb{R}^{256}$.\nA two-layer MLP ($256 \\to 64 \\to 2$, ReLU activation, dropout 0.3) maps $\\bar{h}$ to $\\hat{y} \\in \\{0, 1\\}$.\nThe Stage-1 encoder is frozen during Stage~2 training; only the BiGRU and MLP parameters are updated.\nThis separation ensures that the seven Stage-1 variants produce comparable embeddings evaluated under identical downstream conditions.\nSection~\\ref{sec:decomposition} confirms that mean pooling matches BiGRU, so the detection signal resides in per-turn representations rather than their sequential order."
}
```

To systematically identify what enables cross-attack generalization, we design seven Stage-1 variants that decompose the contributions of domain exposure, classification supervision, label quality, label granularity, and domain specificity. Figure~\\ref{fig:architecture} illustrates the pipeline.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/paper/fig1_architecture.pdf}\n\\caption{DisPeD architecture. ```text
1	% conclusion.tex — D024 compressed
2	
3	\section{Conclusion}
4	\label{sec:conclusion}
5	
6	DisPeD's seven controlled Stage~1 variants establish that continued pretraining is the primary mechanism for cross-attack generalization: MLM without labels bridges the dominant $\sim$62\,pp gap (vanilla 0.28 $\to$ MLM 0.91$\pm$0.12), with near-perfect early detection (F1 $\geq$ 0.97 at $K \leq 5$). Any continued pretraining bridges most of this gap; Wikipedia MLM (0.90$\pm$0.12) nearly matches jailbreak-domain MLM at full length, while jailbreak-domain text provides a meaningful advantage for early detection ($K$=2: 8\,pp gap). Classification supervision adds stability (9-class 0.997$\pm$0.006 vs.\ MLM 0.91$\pm$0.12), perturbation robustness with a three-layer attribution (classification structure provides full-length robustness, label granularity adds $K$=2 robustness, correct fine-grained labels add $K$=1 robustness), and interpretability through strategy trajectories. These results reframe multi-turn jailbreak detection as primarily a domain adaptation problem.
7	
8	\paragraph{Limitations.}
9	The dataset is small (500 training, 160 OOD test conversations) and synthetically generated by Qwen3-8B. TF-IDF achieves F1 = 0.944 on DD; DisPeD's advantage over bag-of-words is strongest under perturbation and for interpretability. Only persuasion-mediated attacks fall within scope. Future work should evaluate on human-generated attacks, scale to larger multi-source datasets, and test robustness against adaptive attacks targeting domain-specific features.
10	
```

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Limitations.}\nThe dataset is small (500 training, 160 OOD test conversations) and synthetically generated by Qwen3-8B. TF-IDF achieves F1 = 0.944 on DD; DisPeD's advantage over bag-of-words is strongest under perturbation and for interpretability. Only persuasion-mediated attacks fall within scope. Future work should evaluate on human-generated attacks, scale to larger multi-source datasets, and test robustness against adaptive attacks targeting domain-specific features.",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Limitations and future work.}\nThe dataset is small (500 training, 160 OOD test conversations) and synthetically generated by Qwen3-8B, which may not capture the full diversity of real-world attack strategies.\nTF-IDF achieves F1 = 0.944 on DD, indicating substantial lexical separability in the current evaluation data; DisPeD's advantage over bag-of-words is strongest under perturbation (0\\,pp vs.\\ $\\sim$12\\,pp degradation) and for interpretability via strategy trajectories.\nThe perturbation robustness evaluation uses $n$=76 paraphrased conversations, where tier boundaries correspond to 1--3 misclassifications; larger-scale evaluation is needed to confirm the three-layer attribution pattern.\nBinary and scrambled variants report single-seed results; multi-seed evaluation would strengthen the comparison with 3-seed variants.\nOnly persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks fall within scope; single-turn token-level attacks~\\citep{zou2023gcg} and non-persuasive multi-turn strategies are not addressed.\n\nFuture work should evaluate on human-generated red-teaming conversations, scale to larger multi-source datasets spanning additional attack families, and test robustness against adaptive adversaries who are aware of the domain-specific features that DisPeD relies on.\nThe finding that continued pretraining is the primary mechanism suggests that scaling training data diversity (more attack families, more generation models) may yield further improvements without architectural changes."
}

_source line 4081 · 2026-05-18T08:08:44.277Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01PJF5GkaxmmdzgfK7Tuhuu7`</summary>

```text
1	% abstract.tex — D024 compressed
2	\begin{abstract}
3	Multi-turn jailbreak attacks against Large Language Models (LLMs) distribute adversarial intent across seemingly benign conversational turns, evading per-turn safety filters.
4	Standard temporal classifiers fail to generalize: a vanilla DeBERTa encoder achieves only F1\,=\,0.28 on out-of-distribution (OOD) attack types.
5	We design seven controlled variants of a DeBERTa-based detection framework to decompose what enables cross-attack generalization.
6	Masked language modeling (MLM) continued pretraining, without any classification labels, achieves near-perfect early detection on unseen attacks (mean F1\,$\geq$\,0.97 at $K\leq 5$ turns) but shows instability at full conversation length (F1\,=\,0.91$\pm$0.12).
7	Any continued pretraining bridges most of the OOD gap (vanilla 0.28 $\to$ $\sim$0.91); jailbreak-domain text improves early detection ($K$=2: 8 percentage point advantage over Wikipedia MLM).
8	Misaligned supervision yields unstable generalization (topic F1\,=\,0.54$\pm$0.21, worst seed below vanilla).
9	The 9-class persuasion model achieves the highest F1 (0.997$\pm$0.006), and classification supervision further contributes stability, perturbation robustness (zero degradation at full length for all classification-supervised variants; correct fine-grained labels add early-turn robustness, $n$=76), and interpretability through strategy trajectories.
10	These results reframe multi-turn jailbreak detection as primarily a domain adaptation problem, with classification supervision providing complementary stability, robustness, and interpretability.
11	\end{abstract}
12	
```

    "originalFile": "% introduction.tex — expanded for 8-page target\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nMulti-turn jailbreak attacks pose a qualitatively different threat to large language model (LLM) safety than their single-turn counterparts. Attacks such as Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door (FITD)~\\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024} distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns through rapport building, logical reframing, and incremental escalation. Persuasion is the common mechanism: \\citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} find identifiable strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks. Individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, rendering per-turn safety filters fundamentally blind.\n\nProduction classifiers such as Llama Guard~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and cannot model manipulation trajectories across exchanges. Recent multi-turn defenses have begun to address conversation-level structure: DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026} aggregates BERT embeddings with a recurrent network, BIID~\\citep{tong2025biid} infers turn-level intentions, and G-Guard~\\citep{gguard2025} applies graph neural networks. These systems achieve reasonable in-distribution performance but degrade on unseen attack types, and none systematically analyze \\emph{why} some representations generalize while others fail. The open question is: \\emph{what representation properties enable cross-attack generalization?}\n\nA vanilla DeBERTa encoder~\\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation collapses from F1\\,=\\,1.000 on in-distribution data to F1\\,=\\,0.284 on out-of-distribution (OOD) Deceptive Delight, worsening with more turns (0.575 at turn~1 to 0.284 at full conversation). LLM-as-judge approaches fare no better: GPT-5.5 achieves F1 of only 0.191--0.449 on in-distribution detection with 30--47\\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\\ref{sec:llm_judge}). Content-level reasoning is insufficient for this task.\n\nWe built \\textbf{DisPeD} (Discourse-level Persuasion Detection), a two-stage framework with seven controlled Stage~1 variants that systematically decompose the mechanism: 9-class persuasion strategy classification, binary classification, scrambled labels, MLM only, topic classification, Wikipedia MLM, and vanilla control (Section~\\ref{sec:method}). Each variant isolates a specific factor (domain exposure, classification supervision, label quality, label granularity, or domain specificity) while holding all other components constant, enabling causal attribution of detection performance to individual factors.\n\nThe result \\emph{partially falsified} our original hypothesis that persuasion-specific classification is the key ingredient. MLM without labels achieves near-perfect early detection on unseen Deceptive Delight (mean F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.97 at $K \\leq 5$), but shows instability at full conversation length (F1\\,=\\,0.91$\\pm$0.12) while the 9-class model maintains 0.997$\\pm$0.006. The evidence hierarchy is:\n\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\noindent\\hspace{0.5em}{\\small vanilla (0.28) $<$ topic (0.54$\\pm$0.21) $\\ll$ wiki-MLM (0.90$\\pm$0.12) $\\approx$ jb-MLM (0.91$\\pm$0.12) $<$ scrambled (0.99) $\\approx$ binary (0.99) $\\approx$ 9-class (0.997$\\pm$0.006)}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\n\\noindent Any continued pretraining bridges the dominant $\\sim$62\\,pp gap from vanilla. Wikipedia MLM nearly matches jailbreak-domain MLM at full length; the domain advantage is meaningful at early prefixes ($K$=2: 0.993 vs.\\ 0.911) and for perturbation robustness. Only the 9-class model achieves F1\\,=\\,0.997$\\pm$0.006 with near-zero variance. This connects to domain-adaptive pretraining~\\citep{gururangan2020dontstop}: continued training captures pragmatic features, with classification supervision providing stability that unsupervised pretraining alone does not guarantee.\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]\n  \\item \\textbf{Systematic decomposition of cross-attack generalization.} Seven controlled variants establish that continued pretraining bridges $\\sim$62\\,pp of the OOD gap; jailbreak-domain text improves early detection ($K$=2: 8\\,pp over Wikipedia MLM). Misaligned supervision (topic F1\\,=\\,0.54$\\pm$0.21) destabilizes generalization.\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Classification supervision adds stability, robustness, and interpretability.} Classification-supervised variants achieve F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.99 with near-zero variance vs.\\ MLM 0.91$\\pm$0.12. Under perturbation ($n$=76), all classification-supervised variants (including scrambled labels) show zero degradation at full length vs.\\ $\\sim$12\\,pp for non-adapted models. A three-layer robustness attribution emerges: classification structure provides full-length robustness, label granularity adds $K$=2 robustness, and correct fine-grained labels yield the strongest early-turn robustness (9-class $\\Delta$=$-$2.6\\,pp vs.\\ scrambled $-$5.3\\,pp vs.\\ binary $-$9.2\\,pp at $K$=1). The persuasion taxonomy additionally provides distinct strategy trajectories per attack type (Section~\\ref{sec:strategy}).\n\\end{itemize}\n",

Any continued pretraining bridges most of this gap; Wikipedia MLM (0.90$\\pm$0.12) nearly matches jailbreak-domain MLM at full length, while jailbreak-domain text provides a meaningful advantage for early detection ($K$=2: 8\\,pp gap). Classification supervision adds stability (9-class 0.997$\\pm$0.006 vs.\\ MLM 0.91$\\pm$0.12), perturbation robustness with a three-layer attribution (classification structure provides full-length robustness, label granularity adds $K$=2 robustness, correct fine-grained labels add $K$=1 robustness), and interpretability through strategy trajectories. These results reframe multi-turn jailbreak detection as primarily a domain adaptation problem.\n\n\\paragraph{Limitations and future work.}\nThe dataset is small (500 training, 160 OOD test conversations) and synthetically generated by Qwen3-8B, which may not capture the full diversity of real-world attack strategies.\nTF-IDF achieves F1 = 0.944 on DD, indicating substantial lexical separability in the current evaluation data; DisPeD's advantage over bag-of-words is strongest under perturbation (0\\,pp vs.\\ $\\sim$12\\,pp degradation) and for interpretability via strategy trajectories.\nThe perturbation robustness evaluation uses $n$=76 paraphrased conversations, where tier boundaries correspond to 1--3 misclassifications; larger-scale evaluation is needed to confirm the three-layer attribution pattern.\nBinary and scrambled variants report single-seed results; multi-seed evaluation would strengthen the comparison with 3-seed variants.\nOnly persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks fall within scope; single-turn token-level attacks~\\citep{zou2023gcg} and non-persuasive multi-turn strategies are not addressed.\n\nFuture work should evaluate on human-generated red-teaming conversations, scale to larger multi-source datasets spanning additional attack families, and test robustness against adaptive adversaries who are aware of the domain-specific features that DisPeD relies on.\nThe finding that continued pretraining is the primary mechanism suggests that scaling training data diversity (more attack families, more generation models) may yield further improvements without architectural changes.\n",

Classification supervision adds stability (9-class 0.997$\\pm$0.006 vs.\\ MLM 0.91$\\pm$0.12), perturbation robustness with a three-layer attribution (classification structure provides full-length robustness, label granularity adds $K$=2 robustness, correct fine-grained labels add $K$=1 robustness), and interpretability through strategy trajectories. These results reframe multi-turn jailbreak detection as primarily a domain adaptation problem.",
          "+DisPeD's seven controlled Stage~1 variants establish that continued pretraining is the primary mechanism for cross-attack generalization: MLM without labels bridges the dominant $\\sim$62\\,pp gap (vanilla 0.28 $\\to$ MLM 0.91$\\pm$0.12), with near-perfect early detection (F1 $\\geq$ 0.97 at $K \\leq 5$). Any continued pretraining bridges most of this gap; Wikipedia MLM (0.90$\\pm$0.12) nearly matches jailbreak-domain MLM at full length, while jailbreak-domain text provides a meaningful advantage for early detection ($K$=2: 8\\,pp gap). Classification supervision adds stability (9-class 0.997$\\pm$0.005 vs.\\ MLM 0.91$\\pm$0.12), perturbation robustness with a three-layer attribution (classification structure provides full-length robustness, label granularity adds $K$=2 robustness, correct fine-grained labels add $K$=1 robustness), and interpretability through strategy trajectories. These results reframe multi-turn jailbreak detection as primarily a domain adaptation problem.",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Limitations and future work.}",
          " The dataset is small (500 training, 160 OOD test conversations) and synthetically generated by Qwen3-8B, which may not capture the full diversity of real-world attack strategies."
        ]
      }
    ],
    "userModified": false,

25	Table~\ref{tab:llm_judge} reports zero-shot performance for GPT-5.5 and Qwen3-8B on the IID Crescendo test set.
26	GPT-5.5 achieves precision of 1.0 at all turn prefixes but recall ranges from only 0.105 to 0.290, resulting in F1 of 0.191 at $K$=2 and 0.449 at $K$=5.
27	A substantial fraction (30--47\%) of responses are unparseable; F1 values exclude these and represent upper bounds.
28	Full-conversation F1 (0.383) is lower than $K$=5 F1 (0.449) because the unparseable rate grows with context length.
29	Qwen3-8B performs worse across all prefixes (F1 from 0.000 at $K$=2 to 0.100 on full conversations).
30	The failure of both a frontier commercial model and an open-weight model confirms a fundamental limitation of content-level reasoning for multi-turn jailbreak detection.

F1 values exclude unparseable responses and represent upper bounds.}\n\\label{tab:llm_judge}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccccr@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Method} & $K$=2 & $K$=3 & $K$=5 & Full & Unparse. \\\\\n\\midrule\nGPT-5.5 & .191 & .273 & .449 & .383 & 30--47\\% \\\\\nQwen3-8B & .000 & .051 & .146 & .100 & 0\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\n9-class (ours) & \\textbf{1.000} & \\textbf{1.000} & \\textbf{1.000} & \\textbf{1.000} & 0\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:llm_judge} reports zero-shot performance for GPT-5.5 and Qwen3-8B on the IID Crescendo test set.\nGPT-5.5 achieves precision of 1.0 at all turn prefixes but recall ranges from only 0.105 to 0.290, resulting in F1 of 0.191 at $K$=2 and 0.449 at $K$=5.\nA substantial fraction (30--47\\%) of responses are unparseable; F1 values exclude these and represent upper bounds.\nFull-conversation F1 (0.383) is lower than $K$=5 F1 (0.449) because the unparseable rate grows with context length.\nQwen3-8B performs worse across all prefixes (F1 from 0.000 at $K$=2 to 0.100 on full conversations).\nThe failure of both a frontier commercial model and an open-weight model confirms a fundamental limitation of content-level reasoning for multi-turn jailbreak detection.\n\n\\section{Full Cross-Attack Results by Prefix}\n\\label{sec:full_prefix}\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Full cross-attack generalization results (F1) at all prefix lengths. 3-seed mean$\\pm$std where available.}\n\\label{tab:full_prefix}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lccccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Method} & $K$=1 & $K$=2 & $K$=3 & $K$=5 & Full \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{IID (Crescendo)}} \\\\[2pt]\n9-class (ours) & 1.000$\\pm$.000 & 1.000$\\pm$.000 & 1.000$\\pm$.000 & 1.000$\\pm$.000 & 1.000$\\pm$.000 \\\\\nMLM-only & .960 & .974 & .987 & 1.000 & 1.000 \\\\\nVanilla DeBERTa & .968$\\pm$.033 & .978$\\pm$.016 & .987$\\pm$.000 & .982$\\pm$.014 & 1.000$\\pm$.000 \\\\\nTF-IDF LR & 1.000 & 1.000 & 1.000 & 1.000 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{OOD (Deceptive Delight)}} \\\\[2pt]\n9-class (ours) & .990$\\pm$.016 & .981$\\pm$.016 & .972$\\pm$.032 & .997$\\pm$.006 & .997$\\pm$.006 \\\\\nMLM-only & .973$\\pm$.019 & .993$\\pm$.009 & .997$\\pm$.005 & .997$\\pm$.005 & .914$\\pm$.115 \\\\\nVanilla DeBERTa & .575 & .438 & .360 & .311 & .284 \\\\\nTF-IDF LR & -- & -- & -- & -- & .944 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{6}{l}{\\textit{OOD (FITD)}} \\\\[2pt]\n9-class (ours) & 1.000 & 1.000 & -- & -- & 1.000 \\\\\nVanilla DeBERTa & .990 & 1.000 & -- & -- & 1.000 \\\\\nTF-IDF LR & 1.000 & -- & -- & -- & 1.000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n",

[Director 指令] [Reviewer Round 2 W1 修复 — K=2 Attribution 逻辑修正]

Reviewer 指出 K=2 robustness attribution "label granularity" 不严密。数据：
- K=2: 9-class Δ=0.0, Scrambled Δ=-2.6, Binary Δ=-2.6, MLM Δ=-4.0
- Scrambled (random + fine-grained) = Binary (correct + coarse) = -2.6pp

Scrambled=Binary 说明 granularity alone 和 correctness alone 在 K=2 都不够。只有 9-class（correct + fine-grained 组合）才在 K=2 维持 0pp。

**修正后的三层归因：**
1. **Full/K≥3**: Classification structure suffices（任何分类 Δ=0）
2. **K=2**: Correct + fine-grained labels required jointly（9-class Δ=0; scrambled=binary=-2.6pp; neither alone suffices）
3. **K=1**: Both contribute independently（granularity: scrambled -5.3 > binary -9.2, +3.9pp; correctness: 9-class -2.6 > scrambled -5.3, +2.7pp）

在 experiments.tex perturbation 讨论、introduction contribution #2、conclusion 中更新此归因。特别注意 K=2 不要说 "label granularity contributes"——改为 "the combination of correct labels and fine granularity is required"。

此修复在处理页面扩展和 std 回退时一并执行。

The Director wants me to fix the K=2 robustness attribution logic. The key insight is:

At K=2:

I'll need to update this analysis across the perturbation discussion, contribution section, and conclusion.

```text
126	\subsection{Robustness Under Adversarial Perturbation}
127	\label{sec:perturbation}
128	
129	\begin{table}[t]
130	\centering
131	\small
132	\caption{Robustness under adversarial paraphrasing ($n$=76). $\Delta$: F1 degradation (pp). Three layers of robustness emerge: classification structure provides full-length robustness, label granularity adds $K$=2 robustness, and label correctness adds $K$=1 robustness.}
133	\label{tab:perturbation}
134	\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}
135	\toprule
136	\textbf{Method} & $\Delta$ Full & $\Delta$ $K$=1 & $\Delta$ $K$=2 & $\Delta$ $K$=3 \\
137	\midrule
138	9-class (ours) & \textbf{0.0} & \textbf{$-$2.6} & \textbf{0.0} & \textbf{0.0} \\
139	Scrambled & \textbf{0.0} & $-$5.3 & $-$2.6 & \textbf{0.0} \\
140	Binary & \textbf{0.0} & $-$9.2 & $-$2.6 & \textbf{0.0} \\
141	\midrule
142	TF-IDF LR & $-$1.3 & $-$9.6 & $-$4.1 & $-$1.3 \\
143	MLM-only & $-$2.6 & $-$9.2 & $-$4.0 & $-$4.0 \\
144	Topic (seed 42) & $-$4.0 & $-$2.7 & $-$2.7 & \textbf{0.0} \\
145	\midrule
146	Wikipedia MLM & $-$12.1 & $-$12.4 & $-$15.4 & $-$17.9 \\
147	Vanilla DeBERTa & $-$11.8 & $-$14.6 & $-$12.1 & $-$13.8 \\
148	\bottomrule
149	\end{tabular}
150	\end{table}
151	
152	Table~\ref{tab:perturbation} reveals a three-layer robustness attribution.
153	All classification-supervised variants (9-class, binary, scrambled) achieve zero degradation at full conversation length and $K \geq 3$, regardless of label correctness.
154	Classification training structure alone suffices for full-length robustness.
155	At $K$=2, label granularity contributes: 9-class ($\Delta$=0.0) outperforms scrambled and binary ($\Delta$=$-$2.6\,pp), which in turn outperform MLM ($\Delta$=$-$4.0\,pp).
156	At $K$=1, label correctness provides the strongest differentiation: 9-class ($\Delta$=$-$2.6\,pp) $>$ scrambled ($-$5.3\,pp) $>$ binary ($-$9.2\,pp) = MLM ($-$9.2\,pp).
157	Correct fine-grained labels yield the most perturbation-invariant early-turn representations.
158	
159	Jailbreak-domain models without classification (MLM, topic, TF-IDF) degrade by 1--4\,pp at full length.
160	Models without jailbreak-domain exposure (Wikipedia MLM, vanilla) degrade by $\sim$12\,pp, despite Wikipedia MLM matching jailbreak MLM on detection (Section~\ref{sec:decomposition}), confirming that perturbation robustness requires jailbreak-domain exposure specifically.
161	The 76-sample test set means tier boundaries correspond to 1--3 misclassifications; larger-scale evaluation is needed to confirm.
162	
163	%----------------------------------------------------------------------
164	\subsection{Strategy Trajectory Analysis}
165	\label{sec:strategy}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_2/projects/persuasion_turn_jailbreak_detect/docs/paper/experiments.tex",
  "old_string": "Table~\\ref{tab:perturbation} reveals a three-layer robustness attribution.\nAll classification-supervised variants (9-class, binary, scrambled) achieve zero degradation at full conversation length and $K \\geq 3$, regardless of label correctness.\nClassification training structure alone suffices for full-length robustness.\nAt $K$=2, label granularity contributes: 9-class ($\\Delta$=0.0) outperforms scrambled and binary ($\\Delta$=$-$2.6\\,pp), which in turn outperform MLM ($\\Delta$=$-$4.0\\,pp).\nAt $K$=1, label correctness provides the strongest differentiation: 9-class ($\\Delta$=$-$2.6\\,pp) $>$ scrambled ($-$5.3\\,pp) $>$ binary ($-$9.2\\,pp) = MLM ($-$9.2\\,pp).\nCorrect fine-grained labels yield the most perturbation-invariant early-turn representations.",
  "new_string": "Table~\\ref{tab:perturbation} reveals a three-layer robustness attribution.\nAll classification-supervised variants (9-class, binary, scrambled) achieve zero degradation at full conversation length and $K \\geq 3$, regardless of label correctness.\nClassification training structure alone suffices for full-length robustness.\nAt $K$=2, the combination of correct and fine-grained labels is required: only 9-class ($\\Delta$=0.0) maintains zero degradation, while scrambled (random labels, fine-grained) and binary (correct labels, coarse) both degrade by $-$2.6\\,pp.\nThe fact that scrambled = binary at $K$=2 shows that neither granularity alone nor correctness alone suffices; the 9-class advantage requires both properties jointly.\nAt $K$=1, both granularity and correctness contribute independently: granularity provides +3.9\\,pp (scrambled $-$5.3 vs.\\ binary $-$9.2\\,pp), and correctness provides +2.7\\,pp (9-class $-$2.6 vs.\\ scrambled $-$5.3\\,pp).\nCorrect fine-grained labels yield the most perturbation-invariant early-turn representations."
}
```

### Tool result

_source line 4162 · 2026-05-18T08:11:31.623Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01NU9PFCJArnRniMqZkqjwb7`</summary>

```text
20	\noindent Any continued pretraining bridges the dominant $\sim$62\,pp gap from vanilla. Wikipedia MLM nearly matches jailbreak-domain MLM at full length; the domain advantage is meaningful at early prefixes ($K$=2: 0.993 vs.\ 0.911) and for perturbation robustness. Only the 9-class model achieves F1\,=\,0.997$\pm$0.005 with near-zero variance. This connects to domain-adaptive pretraining~\citep{gururangan2020dontstop}: continued training captures pragmatic features, with classification supervision providing stability that unsupervised pretraining alone does not guarantee.
21	
22	Our contributions:
23	\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]
24	  \item \textbf{Systematic decomposition of cross-attack generalization.} Seven controlled variants establish that continued pretraining bridges $\sim$62\,pp of the OOD gap; jailbreak-domain text improves early detection ($K$=2: 8\,pp over Wikipedia MLM). Misaligned supervision (topic F1\,=\,0.54$\pm$0.21) destabilizes generalization.
25	
26	  \item \textbf{Classification supervision adds stability, robustness, and interpretability.} Classification-supervised variants achieve F1\,$\geq$\,0.99 with near-zero variance vs.\ MLM 0.91$\pm$0.12. Under perturbation ($n$=76), all classification-supervised variants (including scrambled labels) show zero degradation at full length vs.\ $\sim$12\,pp for non-adapted models. A three-layer robustness attribution emerges: classification structure provides full-length robustness, label granularity adds $K$=2 robustness, and correct fine-grained labels yield the strongest early-turn robustness (9-class $\Delta$=$-$2.6\,pp vs.\ scrambled $-$5.3\,pp vs.\ binary $-$9.2\,pp at $K$=1). The persuasion taxonomy additionally provides distinct strategy trajectories per attack type (Section~\ref{sec:strategy}).
27	\end{itemize}
28	
```

Misaligned supervision (topic F1\\,=\\,0.54$\\pm$0.21) destabilizes generalization.\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Classification supervision adds stability, robustness, and interpretability.} Classification-supervised variants achieve F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.99 with near-zero variance vs.\\ MLM 0.91$\\pm$0.12. Under perturbation ($n$=76), all classification-supervised variants (including scrambled labels) show zero degradation at full length vs.\\ $\\sim$12\\,pp for non-adapted models. A three-layer robustness attribution emerges: classification structure provides full-length robustness, label granularity adds $K$=2 robustness, and correct fine-grained labels yield the strongest early-turn robustness (9-class $\\Delta$=$-$2.6\\,pp vs.\\ scrambled $-$5.3\\,pp vs.\\ binary $-$9.2\\,pp at $K$=1). The persuasion taxonomy additionally provides distinct strategy trajectories per attack type (Section~\\ref{sec:strategy}).\n\\end{itemize}\n",

    "originalFile": "% introduction.tex — expanded for 8-page target\n\n\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nMulti-turn jailbreak attacks pose a qualitatively different threat to large language model (LLM) safety than their single-turn counterparts. Attacks such as Crescendo~\\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door (FITD)~\\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\\citep{deceptivedelight2024} distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns through rapport building, logical reframing, and incremental escalation. Persuasion is the common mechanism: \\citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \\citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} find identifiable strategies in 97\\% of successful attacks. Individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, rendering per-turn safety filters fundamentally blind.\n\nProduction classifiers such as Llama Guard~\\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and cannot model manipulation trajectories across exchanges. Recent multi-turn defenses have begun to address conversation-level structure: DeepContext~\\citep{deepcontext2026} aggregates BERT embeddings with a recurrent network, BIID~\\citep{tong2025biid} infers turn-level intentions, and G-Guard~\\citep{gguard2025} applies graph neural networks. These systems achieve reasonable in-distribution performance but degrade on unseen attack types, and none systematically analyze \\emph{why} some representations generalize while others fail. The open question is: \\emph{what representation properties enable cross-attack generalization?}\n\nA vanilla DeBERTa encoder~\\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation collapses from F1\\,=\\,1.000 on in-distribution data to F1\\,=\\,0.284 on out-of-distribution (OOD) Deceptive Delight, worsening with more turns (0.575 at turn~1 to 0.284 at full conversation). LLM-as-judge approaches fare no better: GPT-5.5 achieves F1 of only 0.191--0.449 on in-distribution detection with 30--47\\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\\ref{sec:llm_judge}). Content-level reasoning is insufficient for this task.\n\nWe built \\textbf{DisPeD} (Discourse-level Persuasion Detection), a two-stage framework with seven controlled Stage~1 variants that systematically decompose the mechanism: 9-class persuasion strategy classification, binary classification, scrambled labels, MLM only, topic classification, Wikipedia MLM, and vanilla control (Section~\\ref{sec:method}). Each variant isolates a specific factor (domain exposure, classification supervision, label quality, label granularity, or domain specificity) while holding all other components constant, enabling causal attribution of detection performance to individual factors.\n\nThe result \\emph{partially falsified} our original hypothesis that persuasion-specific classification is the key ingredient. MLM without labels achieves near-perfect early detection on unseen Deceptive Delight (mean F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.97 at $K \\leq 5$), but shows instability at full conversation length (F1\\,=\\,0.91$\\pm$0.12) while the 9-class model maintains 0.997$\\pm$0.005. The evidence hierarchy is:\n\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\\noindent\\hspace{0.5em}{\\small vanilla (0.28) $<$ topic (0.54$\\pm$0.21) $\\ll$ wiki-MLM (0.90$\\pm$0.12) $\\approx$ jb-MLM (0.91$\\pm$0.12) $<$ scrambled (0.99) $\\approx$ binary (0.99) $\\approx$ 9-class (0.997$\\pm$0.005)}\n\\vspace{2pt}\n\n\\noindent Any continued pretraining bridges the dominant $\\sim$62\\,pp gap from vanilla. Wikipedia MLM nearly matches jailbreak-domain MLM at full length; the domain advantage is meaningful at early prefixes ($K$=2: 0.993 vs.\\ 0.911) and for perturbation robustness. Only the 9-class model achieves F1\\,=\\,0.997$\\pm$0.005 with near-zero variance. This connects to domain-adaptive pretraining~\\citep{gururangan2020dontstop}: continued training captures pragmatic features, with classification supervision providing stability that unsupervised pretraining alone does not guarantee.\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]\n  \\item \\textbf{Systematic decomposition of cross-attack generalization.} Seven controlled variants establish that continued pretraining bridges $\\sim$62\\,pp of the OOD gap; jailbreak-domain text improves early detection ($K$=2: 8\\,pp over Wikipedia MLM). Misaligned supervision (topic F1\\,=\\,0.54$\\pm$0.21) destabilizes generalization.\n\n  \\item \\textbf{Classification supervision adds stability, robustness, and interpretability.} Classification-supervised variants achieve F1\\,$\\geq$\\,0.99 with near-zero variance vs.\\ MLM 0.91$\\pm$0.12. Under perturbation ($n$=76), all classification-supervised variants (including scrambled labels) show zero degradation at full length vs.\\ $\\sim$12\\,pp for non-adapted models. A three-layer robustness attribution emerges: classification structure provides full-length robustness, label granularity adds $K$=2 robustness, and correct fine-grained labels yield the strongest early-turn robustness (9-class $\\Delta$=$-$2.6\\,pp vs.\\ scrambled $-$5.3\\,pp vs.\\ binary $-$9.2\\,pp at $K$=1). The persuasion taxonomy additionally provides distinct strategy trajectories per attack type (Section~\\ref{sec:strategy}).\n\\end{itemize}\n",

1	% conclusion.tex — D024 compressed
2	
3	\section{Conclusion}
4	\label{sec:conclusion}
5	
6	DisPeD's seven controlled Stage~1 variants establish that continued pretraining is the primary mechanism for cross-attack generalization: MLM without labels bridges the dominant $\sim$62\,pp gap (vanilla 0.28 $\to$ MLM 0.91$\pm$0.12), with near-perfect early detection (F1 $\geq$ 0.97 at $K \leq 5$). Any continued pretraining bridges most of this gap; Wikipedia MLM (0.90$\pm$0.12) nearly matches jailbreak-domain MLM at full length, while jailbreak-domain text provides a meaningful advantage for early detection ($K$=2: 8\,pp gap). Classification supervision adds stability (9-class 0.997$\pm$0.005 vs.\ MLM 0.91$\pm$0.12), perturbation robustness with a three-layer attribution (classification structure provides full-length robustness, label granularity adds $K$=2 robustness, correct fine-grained labels add $K$=1 robustness), and interpretability through strategy trajectories. These results reframe multi-turn jailbreak detection as primarily a domain adaptation problem.
7	
8	\paragraph{Limitations and future work.}
9	The dataset is small (500 training, 160 OOD test conversations) and synthetically generated by Qwen3-8B, which may not capture the full diversity of real-world attack strategies.
10	TF-IDF achieves F1 = 0.944 on DD, indicating substantial lexical separability in the current evaluation data; DisPeD's advantage over bag-of-words is strongest under perturbation (0\,pp vs.\ $\sim$12\,pp degradation) and for interpretability via strategy trajectories.
11	The perturbation robustness evaluation uses $n$=76 paraphrased conversations, where tier boundaries correspond to 1--3 misclassifications; larger-scale evaluation is needed to confirm the three-layer attribution pattern.
12	Binary and scrambled variants report single-seed results; multi-seed evaluation would strengthen the comparison with 3-seed variants.
13	Only persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks fall within scope; single-turn token-level attacks~\citep{zou2023gcg} and non-persuasive multi-turn strategies are not addressed.
14	
15	Future work should evaluate on human-generated red-teaming conversations, scale to larger multi-source datasets spanning additional attack families, and test robustness against adaptive adversaries who are aware of the domain-specific features that DisPeD relies on.
16	The finding that continued pretraining is the primary mechanism suggests that scaling training data diversity (more attack families, more generation models) may yield further improvements without architectural changes.
17	
```

</details>

论文文件位置: `paper/*.tex`，docs 副本在 `docs/paper/*.tex`。两边同步修改。

---

## 必须修复（Must-Fix 中的论文部分）

### MF3 (R1-N3): 重构 DeepContext 对比论证
- **位置**: related_work.tex + introduction.tex
- **问题**: 审稿指出 vanilla DisPeD 不能声称是 DeepContext 的 proxy。DeepContext 架构差异显著（fine-tuned BERT, 3-layer GRU hidden=2048, MLP hidden=4096, Focal Loss）vs vanilla DisPeD（off-the-shelf DeBERTa, 1-layer BiGRU hidden=128, MLP 256→64→2）
- **修改方案**: vanilla = "pretrained encoder+RNN without domain adaptation" 类方法的 baseline 代表，不是 DeepContext proxy。指出 DeepContext 本身缺乏 cross-dataset OOD 评估，这是我们的差异化贡献

### MF4 (R1-N4): 降级 claim 强度
- **位置**: abstract.tex, introduction.tex, conclusion.tex
- **修改方案**: 核心 findings 限定为 "preliminary findings on synthetic data"。加强 limitations 讨论数据集规模局限

---

## 应修复（Should-Fix）

### SF1 (R2-W1): Abstract 精简
- 当前 Abstract 堆砌 ~10 个 F1 数字，精简到 2-3 个关键数字
- 保留: 9-class OOD DD F1, vanilla 对比, MLM 的关键发现

### SF2 (R2-W2): 重构 "partially falsified" 叙事
- Introduction 改为正面框架: "我们通过系统消融发现 domain exposure 是主要机制，classification supervision 提供额外稳定性"
- Discussion 可保留假设修正过程作为科学叙事

### SF3 (R2-W3): Contributions 列表精简
- 当前过于密集，拆为 3-4 条简短 bullets

### SF4 (R1-W4): TF-IDF 讨论
- 更诚实讨论 TF-IDF LR 的强表现
- 承认无法区分 pragmatic vs lexical features
- Probing 实验作为 future work

### SF5 (R1-W5): 可复现性补充
- Appendix 补充: Stage-2 完整超参数（lr, batch, optimizer, scheduler）
- 数据生成 prompt 模板
- 策略标注 prompt 模板
- 代码发布计划声明

### SF6 (R2-W7): Limitations 独立 section
- 从 Discussion 拆出独立 `\section{Limitations}`，符合 ACL/ARR 格式要求

### SF7 (R1-W2): 策略标注质量局限
- 在 Limitations 中讨论: Qwen3-8B 标注, κ=0.78, 人工验证仅 n=50
- 承诺数据和 prompt 公开发布

### SF8 (R2-REF1): 补充引用
- Propaganda detection: SemEval-2020 Task 11 等
- Adversarial NLP: TextFooler, BERT-Attack 等
- Probing classifiers: 相关工作

### SF10 (R1-W1): Mean pooling 结论弱化
- 报告 mean pooling 的 single seed 限制，弱化因果性结论

### SF11 (SF12): 图表/格式修复
- 定义 "pp" (percentage points) 首次出现时
- 核实引用年份
- 补充计算成本信息
- 讨论 user-only 假设

---

**写作规范提醒**:
- 禁止 em-dash (—) 和解释性 en-dash (–)
- 禁止 -ing 短语结尾
- 每处修改同步 paper/ 和 docs/paper/
- 引用需要验证（WebSearch 确认真实性）

```text
1	% related_work.tex — D024 compressed
2	
3	\section{Related Work}
4	\label{sec:related_work}
5	
6	\paragraph{Multi-turn jailbreak attacks.}
7	Multi-turn attacks distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns. Crescendo~\citep{russinovich2024crescendo} implements gradual escalation, FITD~\citep{fitd2024} exploits commitment-consistency bias, Deceptive Delight~\citep{deceptivedelight2024} combines emotional manipulation with role assignment, ActorAttack~\citep{ren2024actorattack} uses multi-persona steering, and PAIR~\citep{chao2023pair} automates iterative refinement. \citet{wei2023jailbroken} provide a unifying taxonomy grounded in competing objectives and mismatched generalization. Across these families, a shared reliance on persuasion dynamics~\citep{zeng2024pap,hagendorff2026reasoning,li2024mhj} distinguishes them from token-level attacks such as GCG~\citep{zou2023gcg}.
8	
9	\paragraph{Multi-turn defenses.}
10	Existing defenses model conversation-level structure but do not decompose what enables cross-attack generalization. DeepContext~\citep{deepcontext2026} aggregates BERT embeddings with a recurrent network (F1 = 0.84 IID, fails OOD). BIID~\citep{tong2025biid} infers turn-level intentions, G-Guard~\citep{gguard2025} applies GNN classification, and SafeDream~\citep{safedream2026} predicts unsafe continuations. None ground analysis in the mechanism enabling generalization across attack families. DisPeD addresses this through domain-adaptive continued training, with decomposition experiments showing that domain exposure, not the training objective, is the primary factor.
11	
12	\paragraph{Production safety systems.}
13	Per-turn classifiers (Llama Guard~\citep{inan2023llamaguard,metallamaguard3_2024}, ShieldGemma~\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma}, WildGuard~\citep{han2024wildguard}, Constitutional Classifiers~\citep{anthropic2025constitutional}) cannot model manipulation trajectories spanning multiple turns. LLM-as-judge approaches also fail: GPT-5.5 achieves F1 = 0.191--0.449 with 30--47\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\ref{sec:llm_judge}). DisPeD complements these systems as a conversation-level detection layer.
14	
15	\paragraph{Discourse analysis and computational persuasion.}
16	RST~\citep{mann1988rst}, PDTB~\citep{prasad2008pdtb}, and dialogue act taxonomies~\citep{stolcke2000dialogueact} model pragmatic structure. Speech Act Theory~\citep{austin1962speechacts,searle1969speechacts} distinguishes locutionary from illocutionary acts; existing jailbreak detectors operate at the locutionary level. PAP~\citep{zeng2024pap} identifies 40 persuasion techniques grounded in Cialdini's principles~\citep{cialdini2001influence}, and \citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} find identifiable strategies in 97\% of successful attacks. DisPeD's detection operates through continued training rather than persuasion classification; the taxonomy provides interpretability (Section~\ref{sec:strategy}).
17	
18	\paragraph{Domain-adaptive pretraining.}
19	ULMFiT~\citep{howard2018ulmfit} established pretrain-then-fine-tune; \citet{gururangan2020dontstop} showed that continued pretraining on domain text yields consistent gains across domains. STILTs~\citep{phang2018stilts} and multi-task learning~\citep{caruana1997multitask,liu2019mtdnn} use intermediate tasks. DisPeD's MLM control confirms that continued pretraining is the primary mechanism (MLM F1 $\geq$ 0.97 at $K \leq 5$); Wikipedia MLM (F1 = 0.90$\pm$0.12) nearly matches jailbreak-domain MLM (0.91$\pm$0.12) at full length. Classification supervision reduces cross-seed variance to near-zero (F1 $\geq$ 0.99 vs.\ MLM 0.91$\pm$0.12).
20	
21	\paragraph{Dataset artifacts.}
22	Classifiers are susceptible to spurious correlations~\citep{gururangan2018artifacts,niven2019probing}, and adaptive adversaries can bypass safety classifiers~\citep{nasr2025attackermovessecond,andriushchenko2024adaptive}. TF-IDF achieves F1 = 0.944 on our OOD evaluation; current datasets exhibit lexical separability. DisPeD's advantage is robustness under perturbation (0\,pp degradation for classification-supervised models vs.\ $\sim$12\,pp for non-adapted models) and interpretable detection through strategy trajectories.
23	
```

</details>

3	\section{Conclusion}
4	\label{sec:conclusion}
5	
6	DisPeD's seven controlled Stage~1 variants establish that continued pretraining is the primary mechanism for cross-attack generalization: MLM without labels bridges the dominant $\sim$62\,pp gap (vanilla 0.28 $\to$ MLM 0.91$\pm$0.12), with near-perfect early detection (F1 $\geq$ 0.97 at $K \leq 5$). Any continued pretraining bridges most of this gap; Wikipedia MLM (0.90$\pm$0.12) nearly matches jailbreak-domain MLM at full length, while jailbreak-domain text provides a meaningful advantage for early detection ($K$=2: 8\,pp gap). Classification supervision adds stability (9-class 0.997$\pm$0.005 vs.\ MLM 0.91$\pm$0.12), perturbation robustness with a three-layer attribution (classification structure provides full-length robustness, the combination of correct and fine-grained labels is required at $K$=2, and both properties contribute independently at $K$=1), and interpretability through strategy trajectories. These results reframe multi-turn jailbreak detection as primarily a domain adaptation problem.
7	
8	\paragraph{Limitations and future work.}
9	The dataset is small (500 training, 160 OOD test conversations) and synthetically generated by Qwen3-8B, which may not capture the full diversity of real-world attack strategies.
10	TF-IDF achieves F1 = 0.944 on DD, which reveals substantial lexical separability in the current evaluation data; DisPeD's advantage over bag-of-words is strongest under perturbation (0\,pp vs.\ $\sim$12\,pp degradation) and for interpretability via strategy trajectories.
11	The perturbation robustness evaluation uses $n$=76 paraphrased conversations, where tier boundaries correspond to 1--3 misclassifications; larger-scale evaluation is needed to confirm the three-layer attribution pattern.
12	Binary and scrambled variants report single-seed results; multi-seed evaluation would strengthen the comparison with 3-seed variants.
13	Only persuasion-mediated multi-turn attacks fall within scope; single-turn token-level attacks~\citep{zou2023gcg} and non-persuasive multi-turn strategies are not addressed.
14	
15	Future work should evaluate on human-generated red-teaming conversations, scale to larger multi-source datasets spanning additional attack families, and test robustness against adaptive adversaries who are aware of the domain-specific features that DisPeD relies on.
16	The finding that continued pretraining is the primary mechanism suggests that scaling training data diversity (more attack families, more generation models) may yield further improvements without architectural changes.
17	
```

```text
1	% introduction.tex — expanded for 8-page target
2	
3	\section{Introduction}
4	\label{sec:introduction}
5	
6	Multi-turn jailbreak attacks pose a qualitatively different threat to large language model (LLM) safety than their single-turn counterparts. Attacks such as Crescendo~\citep{russinovich2024crescendo}, Foot-in-the-Door (FITD)~\citep{fitd2024}, and Deceptive Delight~\citep{deceptivedelight2024} distribute adversarial intent across individually benign turns through rapport building, logical reframing, and incremental escalation. Persuasion is the common mechanism: \citet{zeng2024pap} catalogue 40 persuasion techniques across jailbreak families, and \citet{hagendorff2026reasoning} find identifiable strategies in 97\% of successful attacks. Individual attack turns occupy the same embedding region as benign text~\citep{bullwinkel2025repeng}, rendering per-turn safety filters fundamentally blind.
7	
8	Production classifiers such as Llama Guard~\citep{inan2023llamaguard}, WildGuard~\citep{han2024wildguard}, and ShieldGemma~\citep{zeng2024shieldgemma} operate on individual turns and cannot model manipulation trajectories across exchanges. Recent multi-turn defenses have begun to address conversation-level structure: DeepContext~\citep{deepcontext2026} aggregates BERT embeddings with a recurrent network, BIID~\citep{tong2025biid} infers turn-level intentions, and G-Guard~\citep{gguard2025} applies graph neural networks. These systems achieve reasonable in-distribution performance but degrade on unseen attack types, and none systematically analyze \emph{why} some representations generalize while others fail. The open question is: \emph{what representation properties enable cross-attack generalization?}
9	
10	A vanilla DeBERTa encoder~\citep{he2021deberta} with temporal aggregation collapses from F1\,=\,1.000 on in-distribution data to F1\,=\,0.284 on out-of-distribution (OOD) Deceptive Delight, worsening with more turns (0.575 at turn~1 to 0.284 at full conversation). LLM-as-judge approaches fare no better: GPT-5.5 achieves F1 of only 0.191--0.449 on in-distribution detection with 30--47\% unparseable responses (Appendix~\ref{sec:llm_judge}). Content-level reasoning is insufficient for this task.
11	
12	We built \textbf{DisPeD} (Discourse-level Persuasion Detection), a two-stage framework with seven controlled Stage~1 variants that systematically decompose the mechanism: 9-class persuasion strategy classification, binary classification, scrambled labels, MLM only, topic classification, Wikipedia MLM, and vanilla control (Section~\ref{sec:method}). Each variant isolates a specific factor (domain exposure, classification supervision, label quality, label granularity, or domain specificity) while holding all other components constant, enabling causal attribution of detection performance to individual factors.
13	
14	The result \emph{partially falsified} our original hypothesis that persuasion-specific classification is the key ingredient. MLM without labels achieves near-perfect early detection on unseen Deceptive Delight (mean F1\,$\geq$\,0.97 at $K \leq 5$), but shows instability at full conversation length (F1\,=\,0.91$\pm$0.12) while the 9-class model maintains 0.997$\pm$0.005. The evidence hierarchy is:
15	
16	\vspace{2pt}
17	\noindent\hspace{0.5em}{\small vanilla (0.28) $<$ topic (0.54$\pm$0.21) $\ll$ wiki-MLM (0.90$\pm$0.12) $\approx$ jb-MLM (0.91$\pm$0.12) $<$ scrambled (0.99) $\approx$ binary (0.99) $\approx$ 9-class (0.997$\pm$0.005)}
18	\vspace{2pt}
19	
20	\noindent Any continued pretraining bridges the dominant $\sim$62\,pp gap from vanilla. Wikipedia MLM nearly matches jailbreak-domain MLM at full length; the domain advantage is meaningful at early prefixes ($K$=2: 0.993 vs.\ 0.911) and for perturbation robustness. Only the 9-class model achieves F1\,=\,0.997$\pm$0.005 with near-zero variance. This connects to domain-adaptive pretraining~\citep{gururangan2020dontstop}: continued training captures pragmatic features, with classification supervision providing stability that unsupervised pretraining alone does not guarantee.
21	
22	Our contributions:
23	\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*, itemsep=2pt, topsep=4pt]
24	  \item \textbf{Systematic decomposition of cross-attack generalization.} Seven controlled variants establish that continued pretraining bridges $\sim$62\,pp of the OOD gap; jailbreak-domain text improves early detection ($K$=2: 8\,pp over Wikipedia MLM). Misaligned supervision (topic F1\,=\,0.54$\pm$0.21) destabilizes generalization.
25	
26	  \item \textbf{Classification supervision adds stability, robustness, and interpretability.} Classification-supervised variants achieve F1\,$\geq$\,0.99 with near-zero variance vs.\ MLM 0.91$\pm$0.12. Under perturbation ($n$=76), all classification-supervised variants (including scrambled labels) show zero degradation at full length vs.\ $\sim$12\,pp for non-adapted models. A three-layer robustness attribution emerges: classification structure provides full-length robustness, the combination of correct and fine-grained labels is required at $K$=2 (scrambled = binary = $-$2.6\,pp, neither alone suffices), and both properties contribute independently at $K$=1 (9-class $-$2.6\,pp vs.\ scrambled $-$5.3\,pp vs.\ binary $-$9.2\,pp). The persuasion taxonomy additionally provides distinct strategy trajectories per attack type (Section~\ref{sec:strategy}).
27	\end{itemize}
28	
```

1	% abstract.tex — D024 compressed
2	\begin{abstract}
3	Multi-turn jailbreak attacks against Large Language Models (LLMs) distribute adversarial intent across seemingly benign conversational turns, evading per-turn safety filters.
4	Standard temporal classifiers fail to generalize: a vanilla DeBERTa encoder achieves only F1\,=\,0.28 on out-of-distribution (OOD) attack types.
5	We design seven controlled variants of a DeBERTa-based detection framework to decompose what enables cross-attack generalization.
6	Masked language modeling (MLM) continued pretraining, without any classification labels, achieves near-perfect early detection on unseen attacks (mean F1\,$\geq$\,0.97 at $K\leq 5$ turns) but shows instability at full conversation length (F1\,=\,0.91$\pm$0.12).
7	Any continued pretraining bridges most of the OOD gap (vanilla 0.28 $\to$ $\sim$0.91); jailbreak-domain text improves early detection ($K$=2: 8 percentage point advantage over Wikipedia MLM).
8	Misaligned supervision yields unstable generalization (topic F1\,=\,0.54$\pm$0.21, worst seed below vanilla).
9	The 9-class persuasion model achieves the highest F1 (0.997$\pm$0.005), and classification supervision further contributes stability, perturbation robustness (zero degradation at full length for all classification-supervised variants; correct fine-grained labels add early-turn robustness, $n$=76), and interpretability through strategy trajectories.
10	These results reframe multi-turn jailbreak detection as primarily a domain adaptation problem, with classification supervision providing complementary stability, robustness, and interpretability.
11	\end{abstract}
12
